# Web UI inventory: goodvibes-webui to products/webui

Every tracked file of goodvibes-webui (608 files, from `git -C ~/Projects/goodvibes-webui ls-files`), read in full, with its disposition and every check it makes. Nothing is dropped unless it is specific to WRFC or QEMU; the intent document's DROP call for the permission mode sheet and `lib/permission-mode` is void per the owner ruling of 2026-09-26, so those files carry JEV and become the gate presets sheet. No file in this product is WRFC- or QEMU-specific, so there are no DROP rows.

Read, understand, choose (owner ruling 2026-09-27): each row names every check its file makes (each list, set, regex, pattern table, cutoff or threshold, lookup, comparison or classification whose result decides what the code does next), with its line, what it decides and how it decides it today, and the choice made on that reading. A check that answers a question of meaning with a guess becomes a Jev reading, named by pattern and battery; a check stays code only with the concrete reason it is not a guess (id equality, arithmetic, a size or limit the program sets, a grammar a machine produced, a closed enum the daemon or this program writes, a value the owner configured, exactly what the user typed). A category label (deterministic, fixed format, security, display only, renders already-judged state) is never the reason to keep a check as code, and a blanket conversion is never the reason to make one a Jev reading: redaction, sanitizers, pairing and step-up, card and secret detection and format detection get the same reading as everything else. No heuristic is kept behind a Jev reading, not even as a backup, and what a site does when Jev cannot answer is left to the owner.

Where old code tests a closed union the daemon or this program produces with substring or prefix tolerance, the row keeps the check as code and states that the port matches the closed values exactly, so no heuristic survives there either. Every check chosen as a Jev reading also appears in its section's decision-point table (Where, Guessed today, Replaced by). A test file's assertions are not checks: its row lists the checks its helpers and fixtures make and says whether its expectations move because the code under test moves to a Jev reading.

Every file that renders old WRFC-loop state (fleet review verdicts and acceptance checklists, best-of-N attempt judging, the workstream tree and its task graph) is JEV as a view of the contract tree: the value it shows comes from the contract runner's Jev batteries (docs/inventory/wrfc-to-jev.md). Docs that describe the permission mode surface, the workstream view or WRFC are JEV because they are rewritten for the replacement. The UI is not redesigned: every screen, panel, command, style and interaction keeps its look; a Jev reading replaces only the decision.

Files: 608. PORT 548, JEV 60, HOIST 0, DROP 0. Decision points: 26.

| File | Disposition | Note |

| `index.html` | PORT | The Vite entry document: icon and manifest links, each icon URL carrying a literal `?v=1.13.19` cache-bust query (lines 6-10), theme-color and mobile web-app meta tags, the `#root` mount and `<script type="module" src="/src/main.tsx">`. Checks: none; fixed markup with no script logic (the `?v=` values are compared by scripts/cache-bust-check.ts, not here). |

| `public/favicon.png` | PORT | Static binary asset: 322x323 PNG favicon image. Checks: none; image data. |
| `public/goodvibes-icon.png` | PORT | Static binary asset: 322x323 PNG brand mark, precached by public/sw.js line 36. Checks: none; image data. |
| `public/icons/apple-touch-icon-180.png` | PORT | Static binary asset: 180x180 apple-touch icon linked from index.html line 9. Checks: none; image data. |
| `public/icons/icon-192.png` | PORT | Static binary asset: 192x192 PWA icon listed in manifest.webmanifest and used as the push notification icon and badge in public/sw.js lines 197-198. Checks: none; image data. |
| `public/icons/icon-512.png` | PORT | Static binary asset: 512x512 PWA icon listed in manifest.webmanifest. Checks: none; image data. |
| `public/icons/icon-maskable-512.png` | PORT | Static binary asset: 512x512 maskable PWA icon listed in manifest.webmanifest. Checks: none; image data. |
| `public/manifest.webmanifest` | PORT | The PWA manifest JSON: name, short name, description, start URL `/?source=pwa`, standalone display, theme and background colors, three icons. Checks: none; static data the browser reads. |
| `public/sw.js` | PORT | The service worker: precaches the app shell, serves hashed build assets cache-first, never caches daemon data, shows Web Push notifications with Allow and Deny actions for approvals, deep-links notification taps, and re-subscribes on `pushsubscriptionchange`. Checks: L40-44 `NEVER_CACHE_PREFIXES`/`isNeverCache` decides whether a request path belongs to the daemon's live data routes (`/api/`, `/login`, `/status`, `/task`, `/config`); now exact pathname equality or prefix; choice: code (the list is the daemon's own route table, so matching it is the definition of a daemon route, not a guess about content). L51-59 `isCacheableAsset` decides whether a path is immutable build output; now prefix `/assets/`, `/icons/` or equality with three fixed files; choice: code (the paths are the directories and files the build itself emits). L80 activate filter decides which caches are stale; now cache name inequality with the two current names; choice: code (name equality with names this file defines). L91 `event.data.type === 'SKIP_WAITING'` decides whether the page asked for immediate activation; choice: code (a message type this app defines). L99 and L131 `response.ok` / `response.type === 'basic'` decide whether a network response may be cached; choice: code (HTTP status and fetch response type the browser reports). L107 cached-shell presence decides whether to serve from cache; choice: code (presence test). L137 `request.method !== 'GET'` and L143 origin inequality decide whether the worker handles a request at all; choice: code (HTTP method and origin equality). L149 `request.mode === 'navigate'` decides whether a request is a page navigation; choice: code (a request mode the browser sets). L175-177 payload shape guards decide the title, body and data fallbacks; choice: code (type checks on the push payload). L183, L187, L223-243 `data.kind === 'approval'` / `'needs-input'` with non-empty id checks decide which actions and deep link a notification gets; choice: code (dispatch on the daemon's own `PushMessage.data.kind` values). L226 `action === 'approve' \|\| action === 'deny'` decides whether the tap was an action button; choice: code (the action ids this file defines at L202-203). L244 `data.url.startsWith('/')` decides whether a pushed URL stays inside the app before it is opened; now a first-character test; choice: code (whether a URL is same-origin is answered exactly by resolving it against the app origin; the port must compare resolved origins, because the first-character test also admits a protocol-relative `//host` URL). L281 old-subscription options presence decides the resubscribe options; choice: code (presence test). L316-318 `'focus' in client` / `'navigate' in client` decide which window API to call; choice: code (feature detection on the client object). |
| `scripts/cache-bust-check.test.ts` | PORT | Tests `checkCacheBust` (match, stale value with its line, the vacuous no-`?v=` case, several values per line) and the CLI against fixture directories and the repo's own index.html and package.json. Checks: L154-162 `run` helper maps a thrown `execFileSync` error to its exit status; choice: code (reads the process exit status). Expectations are unchanged: the code under test stays code. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `scripts/internal-identifier-check.ts:43-56` (`INTERNAL_IDENTIFIER_PATTERNS`, applied per line at 96-107) | Twelve token-shape regexes (`WS-?[0-9]{1,2}`, `W[0-9]{1,2}\.[0-9]{1,2}`, `wo[0-9]{3,4}`, `WO-[A-Z]`, `DEBT-[0-9]+`, `Wave[- ][0-9]+`, `([A-E][0-9]{1,2})`, slash-chained finding ids and others) guess whether a line of tracked text names an internal planning identifier; the file's own comments record the false-positive trade-offs (function keys, base64, spreadsheet cells) | Jev yes/no per candidate line, battery `webui.gates.planning-identifier`: "Does this line name an internal planning or review-tracking identifier (a workstream, wave, work-order, debt-register or review-finding id) rather than a technical token?"; a yes on any line fails the build with the line cited |

Checks: L81 `rg -n "file:\|link:\|\\.\\./.*goodvibes-sdk"` decides whether package.json or bun.lock points at a local SDK checkout; now a literal search for the dependency-spec prefixes; choice: code (`file:` and `link:` are the package manager's own dependency-spec grammar, so a hit is the definition of a local link). L82 `rg -n "homeassistant\|homeGraph\|HomeGraph\|includeAllSpaces\|knowledgeSpaceId" src` decides which source lines a person must review for Home Graph code; now a literal search for exact SDK identifiers whose hits a person then reads (line 86: "not always errors, but they force an explicit review"); choice: code (the search only lists lines holding those exact identifiers; the person reading them makes the call). L83 `rg -n "wrfc\|workmap\|owner_decision\|owner decision\|route selector\|resume hooks" src` decides which lines a person must review for WRFC or workmap surfaces; same literal-identifier search; choice: code for the same reason. Stays JEV because L83 and the sentence at lines 87-88 ("Do not add WRFC/workmap surfaces...") describe the WRFC surface the contract runner replaces; the step's search terms and that sentence are rewritten in contract-runner terms. |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `vite.config.ts:124` (`daemonSupportsWebuiCommand`) | Whether the installed `goodvibes-daemon` binary offers a `webui` subcommand, guessed by `/\bwebui\b/` matching the word anywhere in its free-text `--help` output (a description line mentioning the webui also matches) | Jev yes/no, proposed battery `webui.devserver.cli-subcommand`: "Does this `--help` listing offer `webui` as a subcommand this binary routes to?" answer yes or no; band set high because a false yes runs `webui status --json` on a build that boots a second daemon for unrecognized arguments |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/components/CredentialStatusPanel.tsx:126-127` (`matched`) | Whether a credential key belongs to the selected provider: a case-insensitive substring test of the provider id inside the key name (GEMINI_API_KEY misses `google`; AZURE_OPENAI_API_KEY matches `openai`) | Jev entity alignment yes/no, proposed battery `webui.credentials.provider-key`: for each credential key K and the selected provider P, "is K a credential provider P uses?", answer yes or no; all keys asked in one fan-out request when a provider is selected; low stakes (a row highlight), so a plain band; the question and band live in the battery file |

| `src/components/fleet/NodeTells.tsx` | JEV | JEV: becomes a view of the contract tree. Renders a node's one-line headline, the stall badge and note, and the review summary (verdict, score, cycles and acceptance checklist) of a reviewed `wrfc-chain` or `wrfc-subtask` node through lib/fleet.ts's readers. In the contract tree the summary is the unit's check summary (`deriveCheckSummary` in the contract fleet adapter): the verdict and each criterion's met or unmet reading come from `contract.unit-judge`, the score from `contract.unit-quality`, and the stall tell from `contract.stall-route`. Checks: L20-21, L28-29, L42-43, L63-64 decide whether each part renders and in which form; choice: code (field presence and the caller's `block` prop). L75 `review.checklist.length === 0` decides the no-checklist note, a failure; choice: code (count; a criterion list with no readings is unmet by the contract rule). L84-90 `entry.verified` decides each item's mark and wording; choice: code (the per-criterion boolean the judge supplies). L91-93 decide whether evidence and how-exercised render; choice: code (presence). |

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/lib/card-material.ts:93` (`isCardMaterialKey`, `CARD_MATERIAL_COMPOUND_SEGMENTS`) | Whether an undeclared config key names a card number, guessed by stripping separators and matching `cardnumber`/`cardnum`/`cardno` | Jev yes/no, proposed battery `webui.settings.card-material-key`: "Does this config key hold payment card material (a card number, security code, expiry or cardholder name) rather than a setting about cards?"; yes drops the key from every settings row source; high stakes band |
| `src/lib/card-material.ts:95` (`isCardMaterialKey`, `CARD_MATERIAL_WORD_TOKENS`) | Whether the key's trailing word names card material, guessed by camelCase splitting and a cvv/cvv2/cvc/cvc2/pan word list | Same reading as L93, one question per key; the exact keys the card-entry surface writes (L90) stay code |
| `src/lib/commands.ts:106` (`scoreCommand`) | Which palette commands best match what the user typed, guessed by a hand-tuned 0-6 tier ladder of prefix and substring positions | Jev rerank, proposed battery `webui.palette.command-rank`: "Which of these commands is the user asking for with this text?" over the registered commands (title, group, keywords), best first; literal presence of the typed text stays code |
| `src/lib/commands.ts:129` (`fuzzyMatch`) | Whether a command that does not contain the query is still meant, guessed by an in-order character subsequence test | Folded into the `webui.palette.command-rank` rerank |
| `src/lib/config-redaction.ts:146-151` (`isSecretConfigKey`, `SECRET_KEY_SUFFIX`) | Whether an undeclared config key holds a secret, guessed by a token/secret/password/apikey suffix regex on the last segment | Jev yes/no, proposed battery `webui.settings.secret-config-key`: "Does this config key hold a secret value (a token, password, signing secret, key material or a reference to one) rather than a setting about one or an identifier?"; yes masks the value; declared SECRET_CONFIG_KEYS membership stays code; high stakes band |
| `src/lib/config-redaction.test.ts:126` (`BROAD_CONTENT_SIGNAL`) | Which real schema keys and daemon paths the coverage test treats as secret-shaped, guessed by a keyword regex anywhere in the key | The coverage test asks `webui.settings.secret-config-key` for every schema key and non-schema path and compares the yes answers with SECRET_CONFIG_KEYS in code |
| `src/lib/config-redaction.test.ts:130` (`KNOWN_SAFE_NON_SECRETS`) | Which keyword hits are not secrets, guessed by a hand exception list | Folded into the same reading; the exception list goes with the keyword regex |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/lib/device-settings-reachable.test.ts:60` (description length) | Whether a device key's schema description states its purpose rather than restating its name, guessed by `description.length > 80` | Jev yes/no, proposed battery `webui.settings.description-purpose`: "Does this description say what the setting does and why someone would change it, beyond restating its name?" |
| `src/lib/errors.ts:76` (`isSessionNotFoundError`) | Whether an error without a machine code means the session does not exist, guessed by `includes('session not found')` | Jev yes/no, proposed battery `webui.errors.daemon-refusal`, item `session_not_found`: "Does this error say the session does not exist?"; asked only when no machine code matched; all items fanned out in one request |
| `src/lib/errors.ts:95` (`isSessionClosedError`) | Whether an error without a code means the session is closed, guessed by `session is closed`/`session closed` substrings | Same battery, item `session_closed`: "Does this error say the session is already closed?" |
| `src/lib/errors.ts:116` (`isSessionActiveError`) | Whether an error without a code means the session must be closed before delete, guessed by `session is active` | Same battery, item `session_active`: "Does this error say the session is still active and has to be closed before it can be deleted?" |
| `src/lib/errors.ts:139` (`isSessionNotLocalError`) | Whether an error without a code means this daemon does not host the session, guessed by `does not host a live runtime` | Same battery, item `session_not_local`: "Does this error say this daemon does not host the session's live runtime?" |
| `src/lib/errors.ts:218` (`isMethodUnavailableError`) | Whether a 404 without METHOD_NOT_FOUND means the daemon does not know the method, guessed by `unknown gateway method` | Same battery, item `method_unknown`: "Does this error say the daemon does not know the requested method?"; asked only behind the 404 status gate, which stays code |
| `src/lib/highlight.ts:115` (`normalizeLanguage`, `LANGUAGE_ALIASES`) | Which grammar a code-fence tag names, guessed by a hand alias table (ps1 to shell, cmd to bash, env and toml to ini) | Jev choice, proposed battery `webui.code.language`: "Which of these languages is this code block written in?" over the 24 registered grammars, with the fence tag as context; a tag that already is a registered grammar name or declared alias stays code |
| `src/lib/highlight.ts:138` (`highlightCode`, `hljs.highlightAuto`) | The language of untagged code, guessed by highlight.js relevance scoring | Same `webui.code.language` choice |
| `src/lib/model-catalog.ts:211` (`detectFamily`, `FAMILY_PATTERNS`) | A model's vendor family for the family grouping, guessed by a regex ladder over id and label | Jev choice, proposed battery `webui.models.family`: "Which model family is this model from?" over Claude, GPT, Gemini, Llama, Qwen, GLM, MiniMax, DeepSeek, Mistral, Command, Grok, Kimi, Other |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/lib/presentation-bridge.ts:73-99` (`classifyBadgeTone`) | Lower-cases any status string and tests three substring keyword ladders in order (`error`/`fail`/`denied`/`expired` bad, `warn`/`pending`/`blocked`/`expiring` warning, `healthy`/`ok`/`ready`/`active` ok, else neutral), so `token` reads ok, `inactive` reads ok and an unforeseen status reads by its letters | Jev choice `webui.status.badge-tone`: "Which badge tone fits this status as a person would read it?" over {ok, warning, bad, neutral}, one reading per distinct status string; StatusBadge keeps its classes and glyphs |
| `src/lib/provider-models.ts:118-136` (`CATALOG_PROVIDER_ALIASES`) | A hand table of 11 runtime provider ids mapped to catalog provider ids (`openai-subscriber` to `openai`, `azure-openai` to `microsoft-foundry`, `dashscope` to `qwen`, `x-ai` to `xai`, ...) decides which catalog provider's model list a runtime provider shows; an id the table does not list gets no catalog match. | Jev candidate selection `webui.models.catalog-provider-match`: given the runtime provider's id, label and published facts and the catalog provider ids the daemon listed, "Which of these catalog providers serves this provider's models, or none?"; the answer joins the explicit catalogProviderId, canonicalProviderId, subscriptionProviderId, baseProviderId, modelProviderId and route providerId values the daemon sends (L137-145, code). |
| `src/lib/provider-models.ts:148-149` (`providerId.endsWith('-subscriber')`) | Assumes a provider id ending in `-subscriber` is the subscription variant of the catalog provider named by the rest of the id, by stripping the suffix. | Folded into the same `webui.models.catalog-provider-match` reading: one question per runtime provider covers both the alias table and the suffix rule. |

| File | Disposition | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `src/lib/voice/capture.test.ts` | PORT | Bun tests of capture.ts: int16 scaling and clamping, re-cutting into 1280-sample frames, resampling continuity, support detection, refusal reasons, device release on every path and the constraints asked of the browser. Checks: L151 navigator and window presence; choice: code. L162, L169-176 AudioContext, AudioWorkletNode and URL presence decide the factories; choice: code (API presence). L167 `isSecureContext === true`; choice: code (the browser's flag). L234 `step !== 1` decides resampling; choice: code (rate arithmetic). L252 empty input is ignored; choice: code (length). L264-283 interpolation position and carry; choice: code (arithmetic). L303-305 clamp to the int16 range; choice: code (sample range). L330-334 worklet channel length and `batch.length >= 8` decide posting; choice: code (a batch size the program sets). L354-365 `classifyGetUserMediaError` maps `NotAllowedError`, `SecurityError`, `PermissionDeniedError` to permission-denied and everything else to device-unavailable; choice: code (DOMException names the Media Capture spec defines). L386 insecure-context decides the insecure-origin refusal; choice: code. L392 unsupported decides that refusal; choice: code. L395 `noiseSuppression === 'speex'` decides the wrong-layer refusal; choice: code (closed request value from the SDK). L409 `request.device` presence decides the exact deviceId constraint; choice: code (the owner's configured device). ### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/lib/voice/request-policy.ts:78` (`SENTENCE_BOUNDARY`, used at L102 in `coalesceForSpeech`) | where an over-long paragraph (over 1800 chars) is cut into sentences for separate synthesis requests, so seams fall where a speaker pauses; guessed by the regex `(?<=[.!?…])\s+`, which cuts after any sentence punctuation followed by whitespace, including "Dr.", "e.g.", "U.S." and an in-sentence ellipsis, and misses an end closed by a quote or bracket | Jev structure recovery, proposed battery `webui.voice.speech-seams`: given the paragraph, return the character offsets where its sentences end; code verifies each offset lies inside the paragraph, then keeps the existing greedy packing and whitespace hard wrap. Answer set: an ordered list of end offsets |

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/lib/pwa/install-prompt.ts:36` (`isIos`) | Whether this browser is an iOS or iPadOS WebKit browser whose only install path is the Share menu, guessed by a regex for iPad, iPhone or iPod in the user-agent string; it misses iPadOS 13 and later Safari, which sends a Macintosh user agent by default | Jev choice `webui.pwa.install-platform` over the user-agent string: "Is this browser running on iOS or iPadOS, where adding the app to the home screen is only possible from the Share menu?", answers ios-share-menu or other; resolveInstallAffordance (L43) shows the Share-menu instructions on ios-share-menu and nothing on other, the same screens as today |
